1|# Theorem P import closure — parent erratum v2
2|
3|Recorded 2026-10-21T04:22:45-0700 (`/bin/date`). Read after erratumv1. Disposes Claude full-proof review /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-18-96h/review/olson-TheoremP-import-closure-claude-v1.prompt.note.md (ad3e8c9b8d52d6445089e1a7163a4ac751ad4fd7279bb1e85e3cf4c72ba9315d):0F/0M/3m, exposition only.
4|
5|1. **Original order:** call the original word AB and the rotated word w′=BA. Cutting after a reflection only cyclically permutes the gap lengths, preserving F,N and sum floor(n_t/2). If P_A,P_B are the selected ordered products in the two parts, the rotated product is P_B P_A and the original product is P_A P_B=P_B^−1(P_B P_A)P_B. Thus the same selected2p positions give identity in the original increasing-index order, including pairs crossing the original boundary.
6|2. **Product formula:** induction from (A,E)(a,e)=(A+Ea,Ee) gives (a_1,e_1)…(a_k,e_k)=(sum_j (product_(l<j)e_l)a_j, product_j e_j). Prefix reflection parity therefore supplies the sign c_t stated in Step4.
7|3. **Notation/degrees:** use degV≤1, degZ≤2 (only upper bounds are needed); write wt(x) for the parameter-vector Hamming weight, distinct from selected length |S(x)|. Write |S(x)|=Z(x)≡0(modp) rather than an equality-to-congruence chain. Codex's exact-degree1 finding is already disposed byv1; weakening the degree2 wording changes no step.
8|

### proof-theoremP.md
Path:/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
Whole-file SHA256:58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
Physical LF:1-109
1|# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
2|
3|Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
4|force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
5|reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
6|prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
7|
8|## Setting and conventions
9|
10|p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11|(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12|For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13|w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14|product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15|k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16|
17|Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18|reflection:
19|    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20|where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21|rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22||X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23|consecutive reflections.)
24|
25|## Theorem P
26|
27|If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28|has an identity 2p-subword.
29|
30|## Proof
31|
32|**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
33|and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
34|the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
35|  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
36|  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
37|Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
38|
39|To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
40|* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
41|* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
42|  otherwise neither;
43|* singletons are never selected.
44|
45|**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
46|parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
47|determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
48|(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
49|selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
50|m_pi = u_t = 0.
51|
52|**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
53|The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
54|even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
55|
56|**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
57|in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
58|it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
59|So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
60|c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
61|rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
62|the product is (V, +1) with
63|  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
64|where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
65|0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
66|u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
67|  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
68|For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
69|c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
70|  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
71|  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
72|V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
73|identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
74|
75|**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
76|integer polynomial
77|  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
78|which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
79|  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
80|On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
81|(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
82|  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
83|(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
84|x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
85|and equal values.)  Hence
86|  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
87|The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
88|V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
89|
90|**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
91|even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
92|even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
93|the identity.  QED.
94|
95|## Remarks
96|
97|1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
98|   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
99|   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
100|2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
101|   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
102|   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
103|3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
104|   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
105|   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
106|4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
107|   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
108|   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
109|   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

24|Mathematically, the following checks support those dispositions.
25|
26|**General NF and GRX.** For an actual all-odd terminal NF,
27|\[
28|F=k+2H,\qquad H=p-1-\kappa,\qquad R=2p+2\kappa-k.
29|\]
30|These use the general normalization paragraph, rather than a later restricted few-odd theorem. Integrality follows from the odd block lengths. Nonnegative \(H\) gives \(\kappa\le p-1\). If \(H\ge p\), the all-plus pattern permits \(2p\) reflections and zero rotations, so avoidance gives \(\kappa\ge0\).
31|
32|For every sign pattern,
33|\[
34|\delta=k-\Phi,\qquad C_{\max}=2H+\Phi.
35|\]
36|Substituting \(n=2p-C\) into general GRX gives exactly
37|\[
38|\max(0,2\kappa+2-\Phi)\le n\le\min(R,2p-\Phi),
39|\qquad n\ \text{even}.
40|\]
41|There is no additional restriction to interval patterns, nonempty gaps, or particular \(T\)-values. Empty intervals impose no test; an admissible \(n=0\) uses \(\Sigma_0=\{0\}\). The interval-boundary formula \(T=y_{j'}-y_j\) has the correct sign under the supplied group convention. See HK 35–52 and `notes-GRX-v1.md` LF18–42.
42|
43|The original relations remain one-way:
44|\[
45|k\le o_F(w),\qquad k\equiv o_F(w)\pmod2.
46|\]
47|They do not establish an upper bound on original \(o_F(w)\).
48|
49|**Theorem P uses gaps.** Its gaps are the actual rotation runs between consecutive reflection occurrences. In this NF, these contribute the \(r_i\), together with internal zero gaps inside constant reflection blocks. Consequently,
50|\[
51|P_{\rm gap}=\sum_i\lfloor r_i/2\rfloor.
52|\]
53|Avoidance and the literal theorem yield
54|\[
55|F+P_{\rm gap}\le3p-2,
56|\qquad
57|P_{\rm gap}\le p+2\kappa-k.
58|\]
59|Thus \(k\le p+2\kappa\). Rotation-value multiplicities play no role in this calculation. The region \(k\ge p+1+2\kappa\) is old coverage.
60|
61|At \(\kappa=1\), every \(k\ge p+3\) is therefore already excluded. The remaining critical family has
62|\[
63|k=p+2,\quad H=p-2,\quad F=3p-2,\quad R=p,\quad P_{\rm gap}=0.
64|\]
65|Each \(r_i\) is zero or one, with exactly two genuinely empty gaps. There are precisely \(p\) maximal reflection runs. A rotation letter \(\rho_0\) still separates reflection runs; it is not an empty gap. HK 54–66 is correct.
66|
67|The all-plus restriction excludes every nonempty even rotation zero-sum of size at least four. Since the odd lift has odd length \(p\), it is either zero-sum-free or contains a zero-sum pair. This dichotomy is exhaustive.
68|
69|**Padding and COLOR.** A skeleton selection uses zero or one reflection from each block. Whichever parity it uses, block \(i\) has capacity for another \(h_i\) pairs. Every total between zero and \(H\) can be distributed among these capacities. Adding those pairs preserves the ordered product. Conversely, reducing each selected block count to its parity removes at most \(H\) pairs and preserves the product.
70|
71|Hence the skeleton’s relevant even lengths are exactly
72|\[
73|2p-2H=2\kappa+2\le L\le2p.
74|\]
75|At \(\kappa=1\), both pure reflection lengths four and six are forbidden for every \(p\ge5\). This argument retains arbitrary \(h_i\); it does not mutate the word or assert inverse avoidance preservation.
76|
77|The COLOR proof also checks. Two doubled labels must interlace, because the other circular arrangements produce an identity of length four. Three pairwise interlacing doubled labels give \(ABCABC\), whose product is an odd reflection product squared. With at most two doubled labels, \(p+2\) positions force exactly two doubles and all \(p\) labels present. The additional label \(C=2A-B\) is distinct from \(A,B\); choosing the \(A\)-to-\(A\) arc avoiding \(C\) supplies \(ABAC\), again an identity. Cyclic cuts preserve the identity condition. HK 70–89 is sound.
78|
79|**The zero-sum-pair branch.** For two zero rotations, repeated block labels provide a \(\Phi=2,T=0\) pattern, and the two zero occurrences supply admissible \(n=2\).
80|
81|For a nonzero pair \(x,-x\), three occurrences of a block label divide the rotation occurrences into three arcs. One arc avoids both marked rotations. Its equal endpoints give \(T=0\), while both marked rotations have the same sign. Again \(n=2\) contradicts GRX. Thus each block label occurs at most twice, and COLOR plus padding finishes the branch.
82|
83|The MINOR issue is that HK 97 and 101 say “reflection value” without explicitly restricting the occurrence count to the block-label sequence. A statement about all reflection letters would not justify this argument and would conflict with retaining arbitrary odd-block pairs.
84|
85|The literal minimal append-only clarification is:
86|
87|> In HK LF97, replace “a reflection value occurred at least three times” with “a value occurred at least three times among the block labels \(y_1,\ldots,y_k\)”.
88|
89|> In HK LF101, replace “every reflection value occurs at most twice” with “every value occurs at most twice among the block labels \(y_1,\ldots,y_k\)”.
90|
91|This changes no case or inequality.
92|
93|**The S(p) cancellation branch.** At the admitted S(p) trust, write the positive odd coefficients relative to \(g\), using E1’s notation:
94|\[
95|u_{\rm one}+s=p,\qquad E=W-p\le p-1.
96|\]
97|Every larger coefficient contributes at least two to \(E\), so \(E\ge2s\). For a larger coefficient \(b\),
98|\[
99|b-1+2(s-1)\le E.
100|\]
101|When \(s\ge2\), this gives
102|\[
103|b\le p-2s+2\le p-s=u_{\rm one}.
104|\]
105|When \(s=1\), the only coefficient exceeding \(u_{\rm one}=p-1\) is \(b=p\). This is precisely \(g^{p-1}(pg)\).
106|
107|Outside that exception, every nonempty bipartition supplies an even signed cancellation:
108|
109|- Ones on both sides give length two.
110|- Otherwise a coefficient \(b\) cancels \(b\) distinct coefficient-one occurrences on the other side, with even length \(b+1\le p-1\).
111|
112|No cancellation uses unavailable copies.
113|
114|A repeated label in two different maximal reflection runs gives two nonempty rotation parts and an equal-boundary \(\Phi=2,T=0\) pattern. The cancellation length lies in its exact GRX interval. Thus the \(p\) reflection-run palettes are disjoint. Since there are \(p+2\) blocks, some run contains multiple blocks; the genuine empty-gap rule makes adjacent labels there different. At least \(p+1\) colors would be required. HK 106–128 therefore closes the whole nonexceptional branch.
115|
116|**The single-zero exception.** Here \(g=(1,c)\) is a generator, so \(c\ne0\), and the actual rotations are \(p-1\) copies of \(c\) and one zero letter. A four-change pattern exists for \(k=p+2\ge7\), independently of whether particular runs are empty.
117|
118|With \(A+B=p-1\), every integer \(d\in[-B,A]\) is realized using \(|d|\) appropriately signed \(c\)-letters. Add the single zero exactly when \(|d|\) is odd. The resulting size is even and at most \(p-1\). The interval contains \(p\) consecutive integers, so the sums \(dc\) cover \(\mathbb F_p\).
119|
120|At \(\Phi=4,\kappa=1\), every one of these sizes is admissible in GRX. This is a complete fresh argument; it does not consume ARC0 or an old zero-multiplicity hypothesis. HK 130–136 passes.
121|
122|The separate \(p=5\) conclusion also passes: normalization and GRX have the supplied \(p\ge5\) interfaces, Theorem P applies, \(6\le2p\), the four-change construction works, and the complete S(p) overlay expressly includes \(n=10\). No p=5 VAC extension, computational lift, or full p=5 theorem is needed.
123|
124|**Top diagnostic.** At \(k=p+2,\kappa=p-1\), \(H=0\), so each block is a singleton. General GRX requires exactly
125|\[
126|n=2p-\Phi.
127|\]
128|Because \(k\) is odd, \(0\le\Phi\le p+1\). All these sizes are available since \(R=3p-4\ge2p\).
129|
130|For CT, the original all-plus lift satisfies \(\mathrm{ZP}_{p-1}\). Taking \(q=p-4\) gives
131|\[
132|R-q=2p,\qquad
133|\sum_a\min(p-4,v_a(Y))\le2p-6.
134|\]
135|Every CT hypothesis is met. This supplies no CT authority for arbitrary signed multisets.
136|
137|The new pairing calculation is valid. If a signed multiset had maximum multiplicity at most \(2p-3\), its \(3p-4\) occurrences would admit \(p-1\) disjoint unequal pairs. The cap-and-opposite-position proof establishes this using distinct occurrences.
138|
139|For \(\Phi\ge4\),
140|\[
141|p-1\le n\le2p-4.
142|\]
143|After selecting one member of every pair, the required number of fillers is between zero and \(p-3\); \(p-2\) occurrences remain outside the pairs. The \(p-1\) nonzero pair differences fill \(\mathbb F_p\) by iterated Cauchy–Davenport. Therefore each such pattern has signed multiplicity at least \(2p-2\).
144|
145|That heavy value is unique. Its original unsigned class contains at least \(2p-2\) occurrences, so different patterns cannot yield different unsigned classes. The common class cannot be zero: repeated block labels would give a \(\Phi=2,T=0\) pattern admitting \(2p-2\) zero rotations. Consequently, at most \(p-2\) rotations lie outside one common **nonzero unsigned pair**.
146|
147|This proves the stated near-pair conclusion. At \(\Phi=2\), the same pairing construction needs \(p-1\) fillers but has only \(p-2\). The argument stops there. It proves neither a general \(\Phi=2\) dominance result nor full rotation rigidity or top-family exclusion. HK 145–171 maintains those boundaries correctly.
148|

150|\[
151|2\le\kappa\le p-1,\qquad p+2\le k\le p+2\kappa.
152|\]
153|R0–R2 retain the actual block and occurrence data, full gap-floor inequality, genuine empty-gap condition, and every general GRX pattern and admissible even size. Nothing restricts the residual to the diagnostic family or to selected frames.

156|\[

158|\qquad q\equiv R\pmod2.
159|\]
160|When this domain is empty, R3 supplies no CT test; it does not remove those \(k\) from HIGH-K-RES. At the top diagnostic the domain consists exactly of \(q=p-4\).

81|## 3. Re-derivation record
82|
83|### 3.1 General NF, GRX and the P-gap reduction
84|
85|**NF formulas.** With k≥2, every block is odd. So F=k+2H, κ̂=p−1−H and R=2p+2κ̂−k. Since H≥0, κ̂≤p−1. Avoidance transfers from w to u only (w→u).
86|
87|**(G).** Because every l_i is odd, δ=#{i: f_i=0}=k−Φ and C_max=2H+Φ. Put n=2p−C. Then:
88|- C≤C_max is equivalent to n≥2κ̂+2−Φ.
89|- C≥Φ is equivalent to n≤2p−Φ.
90|- n is even exactly when C is even.
91|
92|This is exactly LF49. Using an arbitrary z is legitimate because of the global sign symmetry (GRX LF40-41; LO M2). The all-plus pattern gives κ̂≥0 and ZP_κ̂.
93|
94|**Theorem P.** The gaps of u are the r_i plus F−k internal zero gaps, so the floor sum is Pgap. An avoider therefore has Pgap≤p+2κ̂−k. Consequences:
95|- At κ̂=0, P alone already forces k≤p, so VAC is a second, redundant provider. No credit is claimed for it.
96|- At k≥p+2, P alone forces κ̂≥1.
97|- At κ̂=1, P forces k≤p+2.
98|
99|**Critical cell k=p+2, κ̂=1.** Here H=p−2, F=3p−2, R=p and Pgap=0, so every r_i∈{0,1}. That gives p single letters, two empty runs, and p maximal cyclic reflection runs holding p+2 blocks. A ρ_0 is a letter, not an empty gap. ZP_1, together with the fact that odd zero-sums have even length, leaves only length-2 zero-sums.
100|
101|### 3.2 κ̂=1 branches
102|
103|**Padding.** The letters selected from one block are consecutive in the selected subsequence, and σ_y^(c+2j)=σ_y^c. Each block still holds h_i pairs whether c=0 or c=1. The admissible lengths are L∈[4,2p].
104|
105|**COLOR fact.**
106|- σ_aσ_bσ_cσ_d=ρ_(a−b+c−d), so non-interlaced AABB/ABBA patterns give the identity.
107|- If three values are doubled, the only pairwise-interlaced arrangement is ABCABC, and (σ_Aσ_Bσ_C)²=1.
108|- So at most two values are doubled. Then exactly two are, A and B, and all p values occur.
109|- C=2A−B differs from A and B because p is odd, and it occurs once.
110|- On the A-to-A arc that avoids C, the selection A,B,A,C has product ρ_(2A−B−C)=1.
111|
112|A by-product: every critical NF has some value occurring at least 3 times.
113|
114|**§3, Y has a zero-sum pair.**
115|- x=0: two equal block values exist (k>p). Their interval pattern has Φ=2 and T=0, and selecting the two zero letters gives n=2.
116|- x≠0 with some value occurring ≥3 times: three occurrences split the runs into three arcs. One arc misses both marked letters, so both letters land in the same zone and their signed sum is 0.
117|- Otherwise every value occurs ≤2 times and COLOR applies.
118|
119|No S(p) is used in §3.
120|
121|**§4, coefficient split.** Let E=W−p≤p−1.
122|- s≥2: b≤p−2s+2≤p−s=u_one.
123|- s=1: b≤p, and b>u_one forces b=p, which is case (X).
124|- Bipartition lemma: either one coefficient-1 term on each side, giving size 2; or one b-term against b coefficient-1 terms, giving even size b+1≤p−1.
125|
126|**§4, palettes.** If one value appears in two different maximal runs, both arcs between them contain a letter. That gives Φ=2, T=0 and a cancelling selection with n∈[2,p−1], which (G) admits. So the palettes are disjoint. Some run holds at least two blocks, and adjacent blocks in a run differ, so at least p+1 colours would be needed.
127|
128|**§4, case (X).** Here g=(1,c) and pg=(1,0), so Y=ρ_c^(p−1)ρ_0.
129|- Take the minus set to be two nonadjacent runs; this gives Φ=4, which exists since k≥7.
130|- For each of the p consecutive integers d∈[−B,A], select |d| signed c-letters, plus ρ_0 when |d| is odd. The size is even and at most p−1.
131|- (G) admits [0,p] at this pattern, so every residue dc of F_p is reached.
132|
133|**Exhaustion and quantifiers.** The three branches cover every Y. k≥p+3 is excluded by P. The distribution of the extra odd-block pairs never matters, since (G) depends only on 2H.
134|
135|The p=5 case checks out: NF holds for p≥5, P is stated for every odd prime, S(5) holds at Sp-E1 trust, k=7 has nonadjacent runs, and min(5,6)≥4.
136|
137|### 3.3 Top family: k=p+2, κ̂=p−1
138|
139|**Setup.** H=0, all blocks are singletons, F=p+2 and R=3p−4. Every Φ is even and at most p+1, and (G)'s lower and upper bounds coincide at n=2p−Φ.
140|
141|**CT-top.** CT applies with κ=p−1, n=p−4 and R−n=2p. This is the unique (R3) test at the top family. It gives the sum of min(p−4, v_x) is at most 2p−6.
142|
143|**Lemma M step.** Apply Lemma M to the signed multiset with q=p−1; this needs μ≤2p−3. For Φ≥4, n lies in [p−1,2p−4]. The fillers needed, n−(p−1), lie in [0,p−3], and p−2 leftovers are available. Cauchy-Davenport on p−1 nonzero differences covers all of F_p, so T(z) would be attained. Hence μ≥2p−2.
144|
145|**Consequences of μ≥2p−2.**
146|- The heavy value is unique, since 4p−4>R.
147|- The unsigned classes coincide across all Φ≥4 patterns.
148|- The class is not {0}: otherwise 2p−2 zero letters would satisfy a Φ2, T=0 pattern exactly.
149|
150|**Φ2 is correctly left open.** It needs p−1 fillers but only p−2 exist, and the proof rightly treats this as a failure of the argument, not a no-go theorem.
151|
152|### 3.4 HIGH-K-RES
153|
154|**(R0).** κ̂≤p−1. κ̂≥1 holds by P. κ̂≠1 holds by HK1, which rests on S(p); together these give κ̂≥2. The band p+2≤k≤p+2κ̂ also holds.
155|
156|**(R1).** Theorem P, applied with the gap-length reading.
157|
158|**(R2).** The exact GRX criterion.
159|
160|**(R3).** CT on its exact domain.
161|
162|**Reviewer observation (no credit; supports M-6).** Take any datum with the (R0) shape that satisfies (R2). Read as a cyclic word, it is a length-(4p−2) avoider whose maximal cyclic constant blocks are exactly its k odd blocks, so it is its own admissible
163|
164|NF. Hence (R0) together with (R2) is already exact. (R1), (R3), CT-top, the near-pair structure and the existential-NF antecedent are all implied consequences of it (the antecedent is met by taking w := u).
165|

34|
35|Write kappa:=kappa_hat for the NORMAL-FORM parameter, not original kappa(w). NF preserves avoidance,
36|length,F and the rotation multiset from w to u only; no inverse avoidance-preservation is assumed. Its cyclic
37|representation is R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k, S_i=sigma_(y_i)^(2h_i+1), h_i>=0; r_i=|R_i|>=0, and every r_i=0

44|
45|For z in{+1,-1}^k put f_i=[z_i!=z_(i+1)], Phi=sum f_i (even), and
46|  T(z)=sum_(z_i=+1)(y_(i-1)-y_i) mod p, X^z=union_i z_i X_i,
47|where X_i is the actual rotation-value occurrence multiset in run i. Since every block is odd, GRX has
48|C_max=F-(k-Phi)=2H+Phi. Therefore an identity2p-selection exists EXACTLY when some z and EVEN n satisfy
49|  max(0,2kappa+2-Phi)<=n<=min(R,2p-Phi),   T(z) in Sigma_n(X^z).                    (G)
50|An empty size interval gives no test. Sigma_0={0}. This is freshly derived from GENERAL GRX by n=2p-C,
51|not a p+1 lift. For a nonempty proper cyclic interval P of runs, z=-1 on P has Phi2 and
52|T(z)=y_(j')-y_j at its boundary blocks; in particular equal boundary values give T=0.

54|Theorem P applies to u at its literal length<=4p-1,F>=1 scope. Its n_t are rotation-GAP LENGTHS between
55|consecutive reflection occurrences, NOT multiplicities of rotation values. In u these are r_i plus internal
56|zero gaps, so their floor sum is Pgap:=sum_i floor(r_i/2). Any actual avoider necessarily has

62|  H=p-2,F=3p-2,R=p,Pgap=0.

64|REFLECTION RUNS separated by those p single rotation letters. A rho_0 is a rotation LETTER, not an empty
65|gap; do not delete or merge across it without proof. All-plus(G) excludes every even rotation zero-sum of
66|length>=4. Since |Y|=p is odd, either Y is zero-sum-free in C_(2p), or it has a zero-sum PAIR.
67|
68|## 2. Odd-block pair padding and a cyclic color lemma
69|
70|In any all-odd NF, selecting zero or one representative reflection from each block leaves h_i disjoint
71|same-value pairs available in that block, independently of its selected parity. Any integer0..H is a sum
72|of choices0..h_i. Thus an identity skeleton selection of EVEN length L with
73|  2kappa+2=2p-2H<=L<=2p
74|can be padded with(2p-L)/2 pairs to identity2p in u. Conversely deleting selected even pairs from a2p
75|identity selection gives such a skeleton selection. This is selection bookkeeping, not a new word mutation.
76|The skeleton has length R+k=2p+2kappa and a DIFFERENT forbidden-length interval; it is not a p+1 normal form.
77|Cyclic cuts conjugate products and preserve identity in the original index order.
78|
79|In the critical kappa1 family, any pure representative-reflection identity of length4or6 is therefore
80|forbidden (6<=2p for p>=5). We need this elementary COLOR fact:
81|
82|A cyclic sequence of p+2 reflection values over F_p, each value occurring at most twice, cannot avoid all
83|pure reflection identities4and6. Indeed two doubled values A,B must INTERLACE: AABB and ABBA already have
84|product identity. If three values double, pairwise interlacing gives ABCABC up to relabelling/cyclic cut.
85|Its product is(sigma_A sigma_B sigma_C)^2=1, since an odd product of reflections is a reflection. Hence at
86|most two values double. p+2 positions over p values then force exactly two doubles A,B and all p values
87|present, in cyclic order ABAB on those four positions. The value C=2A-B is distinct from A,B since p is odd,
88|so it is present once. The two A-to-A arcs each contain a B. Choose the arc NOT containing C: the resulting
89|cyclic selection A,B,A,C has product rho_(2A-B-C)=1, forbidden. This proves the COLOR fact.               []
90|
91|## 3. Critical kappa1 when Y has a zero-sum pair
92|
93|Let the pair be rotation values x,-x. If x=0, the k>p block values contain two equal values; their interval
94|pattern has Phi2,T0, and the two zero rotations are a signed sum0 for either sign. At kappa1,n2 satisfies(G),
95|giving identity2p, contradiction.
96|
97|If x!=0 and a reflection value occurred at least three times, take three consecutive occurrences of that
98|value around the cycle. Their three intervening run arcs partition the rotation occurrences; one contains
99|neither marked rotation. Its endpoints have equal value, so its Phi2 pattern has T0 and the two marked
100|rotations lie in the SAME sign zone. Their signed sum is0. Again n2 is admissible in(G), contradiction.
101|Thus every reflection value occurs at most twice. The COLOR fact now supplies a pure4or6 identity, which
102|pair padding turns into identity2p in u. This closes the entire zero-sum-pair case, with no S(p) premise. []
103|
104|## 4. Critical kappa1 when Y is zero-sum-free
105|
106|Use the ACCEPTED classical S(p) statement: a zero-sum-free sequence of p odd terms of C_(2p) has an odd
107|generator g with least positive ODD coefficients a_i summing W<=2p-1. This is imported at secondary-restatement
108|trust (Xia-Yuan through Peng-Sun, primary proof unread), NOT claimed proved or upgraded here. Write
109|u=#coefficient1 terms, s=#larger-coefficient terms, so u+s=p. The word 'one' here refers to COEFFICIENT1,
110|not all group generators. Put E=W-p<=p-1. If s>=2, every larger coefficient b satisfies
111|  b<=E-2(s-1)+1<=p-2s+2<=p-s=u.
112|If s=1, b is odd and b<=p. The only case with b>u=p-1 is b=p, namely
113|  Y=g^(p-1)(pg).                                                                (X)
114|If s=0 all coefficients are1.
115|
116|Outside(X), EVERY bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts admits an EVEN signed zero-sum of size2..p-1.
117|If coefficient-one terms occur in both parts, choose one on each side. Otherwise all u such terms are on
118|one side. Choose a term b from the other side and b coefficient-one terms from the first; their signed
119|coefficients cancel exactly, length b+1 even. If s>=2, b+1<=u+1<=p-1; if s=1 outside(X), odd b<=p-2 gives
120|the same bound. The s=0 case necessarily split ones. All choices use actual distinct occurrences.
121|
122|Suppose a reflection value appeared in two DIFFERENT maximal reflection runs of u. Both cyclic arcs between
123|those blocks contain rotations, so they give a bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts. The equal-boundary
124|Phi2 pattern has T0. The preceding cancellation gives a signed even rotation selection of size2..p-1,
125|which lies inside(G)'s Phi2 interval[2,min(p,2p-2)]. Contradiction. Therefore the color palettes of the p
126|maximal reflection runs are disjoint. Each palette is nonempty; k=p+2>p and the genuine empty rule force
127|at least one run to have at least two different block values. The palettes would require at least p+1 colors
128|in F_p, impossible. This closes the nonexceptional zero-sum-free case without imposing rotation rigidity. []
129|
130|For(X), g=(1,c) has c!=0, so rotations are rho_c^(p-1),rho_0. Choose a sign pattern with FOUR changes;
131|it exists on the cycle k=p+2>=7 (take two nonadjacent runs as the minus set). Let A,B count the c-letters
132|with positive/negative signs, A+B=p-1. Every integer d in[-B,A] is obtained using |d| signed c-letters;
133|include the one zero rotation exactly when |d| is odd. This gives an EVEN size0..p-1 and signed sum dc.
134|There are p consecutive integers d, so every residue of F_p is obtained. At Phi4,kappa1, all these even
135|sizes lie in(G): lower0, upper min(p,2p-4)>=p-1. Some selection has signed sum T(z), contradiction.
136|This is a FRESH general-GRX four-change argument; old ARC0's different zero multiplicities are not imported. []
137|
138|Sections3-4 exhaust Y. This proves HK1 for all primes p>=5 at the declared S(p) trust. In particular the
139|agreed p>=7 diagnostic family k=p+2,kappa_hat1 is excluded; all larger k at kappa1 were P-covered. No special
140|k=p+1 lift/window/frame, no arbitrary gap suppression, no rotation-pair or original o_F hypothesis was added.
141|The separate p=5 statement concerns ONLY this kappa1 NF family; no full p5 or finite-computation claim.
142|
143|## 5. The top diagnostic: proved necessary structure, NOT exclusion
144|
145|Now p>=7,k=p+2,kappa=p-1. The verified NF formulas give H0, every block SINGLETON, F=k,R=3p-4. The full
146|residual is the specialization of Sec.6, not merely the tests below. Here(G) has the EXACT rotation size
147|  n=2p-Phi
148|for every sign pattern (all such sizes are available, since0<=Phi<=p+1 and R>=2p). The all-plus odd Y has
149|ZP_(p-1). Accepted CT at n_CT=p-4>=1, |Y|-n_CT=2p, therefore gives
150|  sum_a min(p-4,v_a(Y))<=2p-6.                                                   (CT-top)
151|This is CT on the ORIGINAL all-plus Y only, not on arbitrary signed multisets without their own ZP.
152|
153|For EVERY pattern Phi>=4 a signed value has multiplicity at least2p-2. Here is the complete new-range
154|calculation, not an application of the old k=p+1 Lemma P. A multiset of size N,maximal multiplicity mu has
155|q disjoint unequal-value pairs if2q<=N and q<=N-mu: cap multiplicities at q, whose total is>=2q (the largest
156|keeps q if necessary and the other caps retain at least q); take2q items with each value consecutive and
157|pair positions i,i+q. Equality would fill q+1 equal positions, impossible. This proves the generic pair lemma.
158|
159|If signed mu<=2p-3, apply it to R=3p-4 with q=p-1. Phi>=4 gives n=2p-Phi in[p-1,2p-4]. Outside the q
160|pairs lie p-2 items; choose n-(p-1) in[0,p-3] fillers. Taking one item from each unequal pair gives sums
161|base+sum_(i in I)d_i, with p-1 nonzero differences d_i. Iterated classical Cauchy-Davenport fills all F_p.
162|Some actual n-rotation selection sums to T(z), violating(G). Thus mu>=2p-2 as asserted.
163|
164|The heavy value is unique since2(2p-2)>R. Its ORIGINAL unsigned value class contains at least2p-2 letters.
165|These classes coincide over all Phi>=4 patterns: two different unsigned classes are disjoint and would
166|exceed R. Denote the common class{a,-a}. It is nonzero: otherwise at least2p-2 original rotations are zero;
167|k>p gives two equal block values, whose Phi2,T0 pattern admits precisely n=2p-2 zero rotations, contradiction.
168|Hence at most p-2 rotations lie outside one NONZERO unsigned pair, and each Phi>=4 pattern has a signed
169|value in that pair of multiplicity>=2p-2. This is NEAR-PAIR structure, NOT a claim all rotations lie in a pair.
170|At Phi2, n=2p-2 would require p-1 fillers after the same q pairs, but only p-2 are available: the argument
171|fails by ONE filler. It is not a no-go theorem. No full top-family exclusion or feasible tuple is claimed.
172|

### /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/evidence/import-Sp-XiaYuan-v1.md
SHA256 d4d515cd4ff7b848d703f9337a3e9b6f12f85bd99319173207014519961381d7
LF 1-114
1|# Import note: S(p) as a classical corollary of Xia-Yuan 2010 (Thm 3.1), the index-one theorem and splitting, v1
2|
3|Owner ContinueOlson (math-2 lane, 96-hour continuation).  Written after /bin/date 2026-10-19T03:15:57-0700, on parent instruction (IRC after
4|run review/olson-priority-parent-note-v1.md).  Status: IMPORT NOTE (a known corollary of published results, NOT a lane
5|result), unreviewed by the parent.  It replaces the status 'open' of S(p) (old round notes-SC-lengthp-v2.md; lane
6|evidence/proof-KPS-cond-v3.md) by 'classical import at the trust level of Sec. 2'.  No novelty or priority claim.
7|
8|## 0. Notation and the statements concerned
9|
10|n := 2p, p >= 5 prime; C_n written additively as Z_(2p).  EVEN elements: the subgroup 2C_n (order p) = the even residues;
11|ODD elements: the odd residues.  Every generator of C_n is odd; the only odd non-generator is the involution p.
12|For a generator g and x != 0: |x|_g := the least positive integer a with x = a g (so |x|_g in [1, n - 1]); for a sequence S
13|of nonzero terms, ||S||_g := (1/n) sum_(x in S) |x|_g, and ind(S) := min over generators g of ||S||_g.  For a zero-sum S of
14|nonzero terms n ||S||_g is a positive multiple of n; INDEX 1 means sum_(x in S) |x|_g = n for some generator g.
15|SC (lane; notes-SC-lengthp-v2.md Sec. 0): a sequence U of odd elements is SC if sum_(x in U) |x|_g <= 2p - 1 for some
16|generator g.
17|- S(p): every zero-sum-free sequence of p odd elements of C_(2p) is SC.
18|- IDX1-odd(p+1): every minimal zero-sum sequence of p + 1 odd elements of C_(2p) has index 1.
19|SPLITTABLE (the standard notion used by Xia-Yuan and Peng-Sun Sec. 1): a minimal zero-sum sequence S is splittable if there
20|are a term x of S and y, z in C_n - {0} with y + z = x such that S x^(-1) y z is a minimal zero-sum sequence; otherwise
21|UNSPLITTABLE.
22|
23|## 1. Result recorded (classical import)
24|
25|**Import S(p).**  For every prime p >= 5 (used in the lane only for p >= 7), every zero-sum-free sequence of p odd elements
26|of C_(2p) is SC; equivalently (Sec. 5) every minimal zero-sum sequence of p + 1 odd elements of C_(2p) has index 1.
27|
28|## 2. Imported statements, exact citations and trust
29|
30|(I1) Index-one theorem (existing lane import): every minimal zero-sum sequence over C_n, n >= 3, of length >= floor(n/2) + 2
31|has a 1-element basis {a} (every term is x(t) a with positive least coefficients x(t) summing to ord(a)).  S. Savchev,
32|F. Chen, Long zero-free sequences in finite cyclic groups, Discrete Math. 307 (2007) 2671-2679, DOI
33|10.1016/j.disc.2007.01.012; P. Yuan, On the index of minimal zero-sum sequences over finite cyclic groups, J. Combin.
34|Theory Ser. A 114 (2007) 1545-1551.  Parent import record: run of 2026-10-16, review/olson-index-one-primary-statement-parent-v1.md,
35|sha256 e461514461d23287beeecf04fa26334efd91d3de51caa33312f6c5b10b4d35c6 (author statement read in F. Chen's CANT 2016 slides, p. 4, Thm 1; proofs not read).
36|(I2) Xia-Yuan 2010, Theorem 3.1: Discrete Math. 310 (2010) 1127-1133, DOI 10.1016/j.disc.2009.11.029 (zbMATH Zbl
37|1205.11029; title as indexed begins 'Indexes of unsplittable minimal zero-sum ...').  It classifies the unsplittable minimal
38|zero-sum sequences over C_n of length floor(n/2) + 1 = I(C_n) - 1.  Even-n part as restated verbatim in J. Peng, F. Sun,
39|'On the unsplittable minimal zero-sum sequences over finite cyclic groups of prime order', arXiv:1409.1970v1 (submitted
40|2014-09-06), Theorem 1.3(2), citing [26, Theorem 3.1]:
41|  "If n is even, then either S = (2g)^(n/2-1) (x_1 g) ((n + 2 - x_1) g), where 2 does not divide x_1, ... or
42|   S = g^t (n/2 g) ((1 + n/2) g)^(2l), where ... t + 2l = n/2.  Moreover ind(S) >= 2."
43|  (PDF extraction lost the 'not equal' in x_1 != n + 2 - x_1 and some exponent layout; the conditions elided as '...' are
44|  not recorded.)  Scope confirmed by P. Yuan, CANT 2016 slides 'Unsplittable minimal zero-sum subsequences over C_n',
45|  slide 25: 'Xia and Yuan 2010 (Discrete Math) determined all unsplittable minimal zero-sum sequence of length
46|  |S| = floor(n/2) + 1.'  The zbMATH review is garbled (drops 'unsplittable') and is not used.
47|Trust boundary.  The lane opened no source; it relies on the scout audit run screening/olson-zero-sum-priority-audit-v1.json
48|(sha256 28e9e5678e958d5339912b110ba2bfbe493bf7fdaaf36c0bc8d729d9cc64d93c; findings 0, 1, 3, 9 and theorem_comparison 3) and the parent's note run
49|review/olson-priority-parent-note-v1.md (sha256 6e62cca7d56196792d2cf704eb7b82cd7cd7f3e791e1c33f30e76f21fc0604ed; the parent independently read Peng-Sun Sec. 1, Thm 1.3 and the
50|Thm 1.5 proof).  Xia-Yuan's primary text was NOT read (ScienceDirect HTTP 403; nothing bypassed); its exact hypotheses on
51|n (the audit records an open question about an n = 9 exception) are not verified.  The use here is n = 2p, even, n >= 14 for
52|the lane's applications (p >= 7); p = 5 (n = 10) is covered only if Thm 3.1 includes n = 10.
53|The splitting monotonicity (stated in Peng-Sun Sec. 1 and the proof of their Thm 1.5, and in Gao-Li-Yuan-Zhuang, Arch.
54|Math. 105 (2015) 361-370, Sec. 1) is NOT imported: it is proved in Sec. 3.
55|
56|## 3. Lemma SPLIT (proved here)
57|
58|Let M be a minimal zero-sum sequence over C_n and M' := M x^(-1) y z with y + z = x, y, z != 0, M' a minimal zero-sum
59|sequence.  Then ||M||_g <= ||M'||_g for every generator g; hence ||M'||_g = 1 implies ||M||_g = 1.
60|Proof.  |y|_g + |z|_g is a positive integer congruent to |x|_g modulo n, and |x|_g is the least positive integer in its
61|class, so |x|_g <= |y|_g + |z|_g; summing, n ||M||_g <= n ||M'||_g.  If n ||M'||_g = n, then n ||M||_g is a positive multiple
62|of n (M is a zero-sum of nonzero terms) that is <= n, so it equals n.  []
63|
64|## 4. Parity check of both Xia-Yuan families at n = 2p (the only property of (I2) used)
65|
66|Claim P.  Every unsplittable minimal zero-sum sequence S of length p + 1 over C_(2p) that consists of odd elements is
67|g^p (pg) for a generator g, and has index 1.
68|Proof.  By (I2) (n = 2p even, floor(n/2) + 1 = p + 1), S belongs to one of the two families.
69|- Family A, (2g)^(p-1) (x_1 g) ((2p + 2 - x_1) g): the term 2g lies in 2C_(2p) for EVERY g (generator or not) and occurs
70|  p - 1 >= 4 times; so S contains an even element: excluded.
71|- Family B, g^t (pg) ((p + 1) g)^(2l) with t + 2l = p: (p + 1) g = 2 ((p + 1)/2) g lies in 2C_(2p) for every g; if l >= 1, S
72|  contains an even element: excluded.  If l = 0 (whether or not the elided conditions exclude it), S = g^p (pg).  All terms
73|  odd forces g odd; g = p would give S = p^(p+1), which contains the proper zero-sum p p: not minimal.  So g is a generator,
74|  and sum |.|_g = p * 1 + p = 2p: index 1.  []
75|(With the full statement 'ind(S) >= 2' no all-odd unsplittable S of length p + 1 exists at all; this is not needed.)
76|
77|## 5. Derivation of IDX1-odd(p+1) and of S(p)
78|
79|Theorem.  For every prime p >= 5 (n = 2p within the range of (I2)), every minimal zero-sum sequence M of p + 1 odd elements
80|of C_(2p) has index 1.
81|Proof.  If M is unsplittable, Claim P.  If M is splittable, M' := M x^(-1) y z is a minimal zero-sum sequence of length
82|p + 2 = floor(2p/2) + 2, so by (I1) it has a 1-element basis {a}.  M' contains the p >= 5 odd terms of M other than x;
83|an odd element is not a multiple of an even element, so a is odd; a = p would make every term of M' equal to p, and p p
84|would be a proper zero-sum of M' (length p + 2 > 2): impossible.  So a is a generator and ||M'||_a = 1; Lemma SPLIT gives
85|||M||_a = 1.  []
86|Equivalence (lane, evidence/notes-Sp-IDX1-v1.md, sha256 7af84f0d10670594c90274af9d9a70bc2e4a067b7b59a4e691d8a72500d31e53; repeated in full).
87|(IDX1-odd(p+1) => S(p)) Let U be zero-sum-free, p odd terms; x := -sigma(U) is odd (a sum of p odd elements is odd), so
88|x != 0.  M := U x is a zero-sum of length p + 1, minimal: a proper nonempty zero-sum avoiding x lies in U; one containing x
89|leaves a nonempty zero-sum M - S inside U.  Index 1 of M with generator g gives sum_U |.|_g = 2p - |x|_g <= 2p - 1: U is SC.
90|(S(p) => IDX1-odd(p+1)) For M all-odd minimal of length p + 1 and a term m, U := M - m is zero-sum-free of length p; SC
91|with g gives m = -sigma(U) = (2p - sum_U |.|_g) g, so |m|_g = 2p - sum_U |.|_g and sum_M |.|_g = 2p.  []
92|Hence Import S(p) holds at the trust level of Sec. 2.
93|
94|## 6. Controls and cross-checks (NOT premises)

96|- Involution case without any import: if U contains pg, then U = g^(p-1) (pg) (parent's argument, checked in
97|  evidence/notes-Sp-involution-reduction-v1.md, sha256 233b1cb1a1815ba0e5793a97b322104d8299ed75f408661aca1844e31abd843f); the import is needed only for sequences of units.
98|- Lane probe v13 (code/sp_falsify_probe_v13.py 9b0d6a7aab239c1b4fa8cb5453afc090cdc00219b83689fb16623ff5ab1a6e88, gated launcher, pid 57453, rc 0; recorded in
99|  notes-Sp-IDX1-v1.md): complete canonical enumeration, every zero-sum-free odd sequence of length p is SC for
100|  p = 5, 7, 11, 13, 17.
101|- Parent finite control run review/olson-sp-import-parent-control-v1.json (sha256 133396151e311a6b51993c29f8f6bb35bfd0646387bb1516dd5cb8d84fa9860b; status
102|  PARENT_FINITE_CONTROL_NOT_PREMISE): exhaustive all-odd minimal zero-sums of length p + 1 over C_(2p): p = 5: 8, p = 7: 18,
103|  p = 11: 70, every one of index 1; the all-odd minimal sequence g^(p-4) ((p+2) g)^2 (pg) of length p - 1 has index 2 for
104|  p = 7, 11, 13, 17, 19 (index 1 at p = 5).  So index 1 can fail for all-odd minimal zero-sums just below length p + 1, and
105|  for general sequences at length p + 1 (Family B with l >= 1, e.g. the parent's 1^(p-2) p (p+1)^2): the all-odd

6|

The group convention is(v,e)(v',e')=(v+ev',ee'), rho_x=(x,+1),sigma_y=(y,-1); identity selections have even reflection parity.

14|## Exact accepted statements
15|
16|An avoider has no identity 2p-subword in increasing original index order. For a word w, F counts reflections, o=o_F counts odd blocks in the **maximal cyclic constant reflection-block decomposition**, and κ(w)=p−1−(F−o)/2. INV* means all rotations lie in one nonzero pair {ρ_c,ρ_−c} and every maximal cyclic reflection run is constant.
17|
18|1. **ZX.** Let k,κ be integers, k≥1, 3k≤2p+1 and 0≤κ≤k−1. If Y is a sequence of 2p+2κ−k odd elements of Z_(2p) and every zero-sum subsequence has length ≤2κ, choose a **longest** zero-sum Z and a value g of maximum multiplicity in Y−Z. Then g is a generator and cost_g(Y)≤e=k−1−2κ+|Z|≤k−1. Every signed odd coefficient b_g satisfies |b_g|≤k; positive terms of Z equal g; E+N=cost_g(Y).
19|2. **S1(a), S1(b), S1(c), and S1+.** For integer κ≥0, let (ZP) exclude zero-sums of lengths [2κ+2,2p], and let (S1) bound every zero-sum length by 2κ. Part (a) gives (S1) when κ≤(p−1)/2. Part (c) also gives it at κ=(p+1)/2. Thus **(ZP) implies (S1) for all integers 0≤κ≤(p+1)/2**. Separately, under 6κ≤4p+2, failure of (S1) supplies two disjoint minimal zero-sums P,P′ of lengths in [2p−2κ+2,2κ] with |P|+|P′|≥2p+2. Part (b) describes an obstruction to (S1), not a counterexample to ROT/INV*/Olson.
20|3. **Terminal ROT-ZX.** For any length-(4p−2) avoider, it suffices that **some** normal form produced by the accepted process has k=1, or has 3k≤2p+1 and (S1) for its rotation sequence at κ̂=p−1−(F−k)/2. Then the original rotations lie in one nonzero pair. In particular, **κ̂≤(p+1)/2** suffices in the second branch. No uniqueness of normal form is assumed. To infer INV* for the original word from this terminal criterion, retain the extra condition o_F(w)≤p.
LF 49-61

1|# Olson sequence delta: LS-II', LS-small, BASE1, LS+2, LS-lin, CT and CTgen
2|
3|Recorded at 2026-10-19T06:52:35-0700.
4|
5|**CORRECTNESS_ACCEPTED_SCOPED_PARTIAL**, relative to the exact accepted imports below. These are sequence-level written results, not a proof of unrestricted LS, LS+, STAB, INV* or ordered Olson. No finite computation or Lean verification was performed for this verdict.
6|
7|## Definitions and governing texts
8|
9|Y is a sequence of odd elements of C_(2p). (ZP_kappa) forbids zero-sum subsequences of even lengths from 2kappa+2 through 2p. Under this condition a long zero-sum has length at least 2p+2. Put kappa0=floor(2p/3), e=p-kappa. For the new-range LS/LS+/STAB claims below, p is prime >=7 and kappa0+1 <= kappa <= p-2 unless a narrower range is stated.
10|
11|Read the [delta package](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/delta-review-package-v1.md) through mandatory [AP-prime erratum](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/erratum-1-delta-APprime.md) and [consolidated delta erratum](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/erratum-2-delta-consolidated.md) `16cc18235f4353605ebd55753de4304bcf80e5b2c18b42f6fa0c68117795cc27`. CF/AP also require the [prerequisite consolidated erratum](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/erratum-2-prereq-consolidated.md), especially R4/R6.
12|
13|## Accepted results
14|
15|1. **LS-II'.** No threshold LS counterexample (odd Y of length p+2kappa+1, with (ZP_kappa) and a long zero-sum) lies in case (II) with respect to g=g0 of DGM-p. The former induction premise H'(kappa) and Lemma SH are not used; declared accepted imports still are.
16|2. **LS-small and Proposition I-small.** LS(p,p-2) for p>=7; LS(p,p-3) for p>=11; LS(p,p-4) for p>=13. Proposition I-small is accepted only with its own literal hypotheses and corrected AP-prime proof.
17|3. **BASE1 / BASE1+.** For p congruent to 1 mod3, p>=7, kappa=(2p+1)/3, an odd (ZP_kappa) sequence of length 3kappa+1=2p+2 containing a long zero-sum is exactly
18|   - g^(p+3)(3g)^(p-1), or
19|   - g^(2p-1)(cg)^3, where c=(2p+1)/3=3^(-1) mod2p,
20|   for an odd generator g. Any odd (ZP_kappa) sequence containing a long zero-sum has length <=2p+2. This is STAB and LS+ at these cells only; the first p congruent to 2 mod3 cell is not covered.
21|4. **LS+2.** At kappa=p-2, p>=7, every odd (ZP_kappa) sequence containing a long zero-sum has length <=3p-5=3kappa+1. The stated E2/E3 families attain this bound. This does not classify every endpoint extremizer (general STAB(p,p-2) is not proved).
22|5. **LS-lin.** For e>=3 and p>=6e-10, LS(p,kappa) holds. Together with the accepted e=2 cell, the linear band is e<=(p+10)/6, always restricted to the new kappa range above.
23|6. **CT.** For prime p>=5, 0<=kappa<=p-1, odd (ZP_kappa) Y, and 1<=n<=|Y| with |Y|-n even in [2kappa+2,2p], sum_x min(n,v_x(Y)) <= p+n-2.
24|7. **CTgen.** For prime p>=5, (p-3)/2<=kappa<=p-1, N=|Y|>=2kappa+3, odd (ZP_kappa) Y containing a long zero-sum: the involution p has multiplicity <=N-2kappa-2 and some odd generator g has multiplicity >=N-2kappa-1. At N=3kappa+2 this supplies v_g>=kappa+1 to the restricted ROW dichotomy; it is not itself a proof of LS+.
25|
26|## Exact combined cells and remainder
27|
28|Within the stated new range, accepted LS cells are the union of:
29|- e<=(p+10)/6;
30|- e<=4 (including the standalone LS-small cell (13,9), also BASE1);
31|- kappa=(2p+1)/3 when p congruent to1 mod3.
32|
33|Cells not decided by this package have e>=5, p<6e-10, excluding those BASE1 cells. Examples: (p,kappa)=(17,12),(19,14),(23,16),(23,17). This describes the package's coverage, not a literature-wide open-status theorem. LS+ is accepted here only at e=2 and BASE1 cells; STAB only at BASE1 cells.

7|## D1: Lemma AP' (statement unchanged), corrected proof
8|Let n >= 1, 3n + 2 < p, d := |x~| in [1, (p - 1)/2], I an arc of l <= 2n + 1 consecutive residues containing the n + 1 points
9|a + rho x (0 <= rho <= n).  The n distinct points with 1 <= rho <= n lie in I n (I + x), whose size is
10|max(0, l - d) + max(0, l - p + d).
11|(i) d >= l: l - d <= 0 and l - p + d <= 2d - p < 0, so the intersection is EMPTY: contradiction.
12|(ii) p - l < d < l: l - d < 2l - p and l - p + d < 2l - p, and if both are positive their sum is 2l - p; as 2l - p <= 4n + 2 - p < n,
13|the intersection has fewer than n points: contradiction.
14|(iii) Otherwise d <= p - l and d < l, so l - p + d <= 0 and n <= l - d: d <= l - n <= n + 1.
15|Lifting (induction): L = [alpha, alpha + l - 1] maps onto I, y_0 in L lifts a, y_rho := y_0 + rho x~.  If y_rho is in L then
16|y_(rho+1) is in [alpha - n - 1, alpha + 3n + 1] and its residue is in I, so y_(rho+1) is in L + mp; m >= 1 is impossible as
17|p > 3n + 1, and m <= -1 is impossible as l + n < p.  So y_n, y_0 are in L, n d <= l - 1 <= 2n, d <= 2: x in {1, -1, 2, -2}.  []

20|Lemma AP in erratum-2-prereq-consolidated.md R6 governs there.  proof-STAB-base-p1mod3-v1.md and proof-LSplus-e2-v1.md use neither
21|AP nor AP'.

93|
94|At k=p+1 and (p-1)/2<=kappa^<=p-2, CT at n=p-3 has R-(p-3)=2kappa^+2 in its window and capped sum<=2p-5. LemmaU gives delta<=p-2. Arrange A>=A'. 49|1.3 Admissible normal form [row NF]. Let w be an avoider of length 4p-2. Then F >= 1 (F = 0 would give 4p-2 >= 3p-1
50|rotations and EGZ twice an identity 2p-subword). Cut every maximal cyclic reflection run into its maximal constant
51|sub-blocks (empty rotation runs between them); o := o_F(w) counts the odd ones. Apply PM* across every even block and read two
52|equal-valued blocks separated by an empty run as one block, repeating; each step lowers the odd-block count by 0 or 2 and the
53|block count by at least 1, so the process terminates. Any terminal word w^ of this (in general non-unique) process is an
54|ADMISSIBLE normal form; by ZX overlay 2 every criterion below is existential ("some admissible normal form") and every residual
55|statement must quantify over ALL admissible normal forms. w^ is an avoider (PM* preserves avoidance from original to reduced),
56|has length 4p-2, the same F and the same rotation multiset as w, and is EITHER a cyclic constant-block representation with
57|1 <= k <= o odd blocks, k = o (mod 2), every empty run separating blocks of different values, OR one even block. The even-block
58|ending is impossible for an avoider (Proposition K1 forces F = 2p-1, odd; lowodd overlay M4). Hence every avoider of length
59|4p-2 has at least one admissible normal form, and every admissible normal form has terminal 1 <= k <= o_F(w).

### /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/proof-ROT-few-E-v4.md
SHA256 ac560eb81b0e17af5cad69345d7498fd431bc8f3c7e94b56701509983cb9ea8f
LF 28-43
28|## 0. Representation
29|
30|Split every maximal cyclic reflection run of an avoider w into its maximal constant sub-blocks; consecutive sub-blocks
31|of one run are separated by EMPTY rotation runs and have different values.  o := the number of odd sub-blocks.
32|Normal form: F >= 1, since F = 0 would leave R = 4p-2 >= 3p-1 rotations and EGZ (twice) would give an identity
33|2p-subword.  Apply PM* across every even sub-block (it allows empty runs); whenever two blocks of the same value become
34|separated by an empty run, read them as one block (its length is the sum of theirs, so it is odd exactly when exactly
35|one of them is odd) and apply PM* again if it is even; each such step lowers the number of odd blocks by 2 or leaves it
36|unchanged (e.g. A | E1 | E2 with A odd, E1, E2 even and E2 of A's value: PM* across E1 lets A and E2 fuse into one
37|odd block), so k <= o and k = o (mod 2).  The process ends with an avoider w^ of the same length and rotation multiset, given EITHER by a cyclic
38|representation with 1 <= k <= o blocks, all odd, in which every EMPTY run separates two blocks of DIFFERENT values,
39|OR (no odd block left, possibly after an equal-valued merge) by ONE EVEN block.  The even-block case is impossible:
40|accepted Proposition K1 forces F = 2p-1, which is odd (equivalently, Davenport-alpha with H = F/2 and F >= 2 gives
41|R + F/2 <= 3p-2, i.e. F/2 >= p, and 2p reflections of that block form an identity 2p-subword).  If k = 1, Proposition
42|K1 makes all rotations equal.  So let k >= 2.  As before H = (F - k)/2, kappa = p - 1 - H, R = 2p + 2kappa - k, and Lemma DAV
43|(boundary gaps are never in I; the frame is a boundary gap) gives kappa <= k - 1; GRX gives (ZP).

### /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/notes-GRX-v1.md
SHA256 afc6c31a2f9c9502655e3666595e535f4a003ccd7670ddbe993ebe4b620b1a30
LF 7-42
7|## 1. Representation and statement
8|
9|p odd prime, conventions of olson/proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 0.  Let w have F >= 1 reflections.  Read w
10|cyclically and cut every maximal cyclic reflection run at each change of value: w is a cyclic alternation
11|    R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k,   S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i) (l_i >= 1),   R_i = rotations with value multiset X_i,
12||X_i| = r_i >= 0 (R_i may be EMPTY; then y_(i-1) != y_i when the blocks are maximal, but the criterion below holds
13|for every such representation, maximal or not).  y_0 := y_k, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i, sum theta_i = 0.
14|For z in {+-1}^k: f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)] (z_(k+1) = z_1), Phi = sum f_i (even), delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2},
15|C_max = F - delta, X^z := multiset union over i of z_i X_i (each value multiplied by z_i), R = sum r_i,
16|T(z) := sum_{i : z_i = +1} theta_i (mod p), and Sigma_m(Y) := set of sums of size-m sub-multisets of Y.
17|
18|**Lemma GRX.**  w has an identity 2p-subword iff there are z and an even C with Phi <= C <= C_max, 0 <= 2p - C <= R
19|and T(z) in Sigma_(2p-C)(X^z).
20|(F = 0: identity 2p-subword iff 0 in Sigma_(2p)(X); rotations commute.  The sign pattern may be normalised z_1 = +1.)
21|
22|## 2. Proof
23|
24|A selection with an even number of reflections is given by (i) z_i := (-1)^(number of selected reflections
25|cyclically before R_i, counted from a fixed cut point at the end of S_k), (ii) c_i := number of selected letters of
26|S_i, with c_i = f_i (mod 2) [the parity of selected reflections in S_i is exactly what changes the zone sign between
27|R_i and R_(i+1)], (iii) any sub-multiset of each X_i.  Conversely each such datum with 0 <= c_i <= l_i is realised
28|(choose any c_i letters of the constant block S_i; any chosen rotations).  Because S_i is constant, its c_i selected
29|letters multiply to sigma_(y_i) (c_i odd) or 1 (c_i even); so the ordered product, read from the cut, is
30|    rho_V,  V = sum_i z_i (sum of chosen values in X_i) + sum_{i : f_i = 1} z_i y_i      (mod p),
31|by induction along the word (a rotation rho_x in a zone of sign z contributes z x; a reflection sigma_y entered in
32|a zone of sign z contributes z y and flips the zone sign; the even total makes the product a rotation).
33|Summation by parts: sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_(i+1))/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2 = -(sum_i z_i theta_i)/2,
34|and sum_i z_i theta_i = 2 sum_{z_i=+1} theta_i - sum_i theta_i = 2 T(z).  Hence V = sigma - T(z), sigma := the sum of
35|the chosen elements of X^z.  The size is C + m with C = sum c_i, m = number of chosen rotations.  For fixed z the
36|realisable C are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max]: c_i ranges over the integers in [0, l_i] congruent to
37|f_i mod 2, i.e. from f_i up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; summing, C ranges over all values of the right parity
38|between Phi and F - delta (each c_i moves in steps of 2), and Phi + C_max = F - delta + Phi is even because F = sum l_i
39|= delta + Phi (mod 2).  The rotations are chosen independently of the c_i.  So the selection is an identity
40|2p-subword iff C + m = 2p and sigma = T(z), as claimed.  The global sign change z -> -z maps X^z -> -X^z and
41|T(z) -> sum_{z_i=-1} theta_i = -T(z), so z_1 = +1 is a normalisation.  Identity subwords are invariant under cyclic
42|rotation of w (UV = 1 iff VU = 1), so the cyclic reading is exact.  []

### /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
SHA256 58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
LF 8-28
8|## Setting and conventions
9|
10|p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11|(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12|For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13|w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14|product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15|k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16|
17|Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18|reflection:
19|    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20|where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21|rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22||X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23|consecutive reflections.)
24|
25|## Theorem P
26|
27|If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28|has an identity 2p-subword.

86|## 3. Proof of Proposition VAC
87|
88|Let k >= p+1 and suppose kappa^ = 0. The k block values y_i lie in Z_p, so y_j = y_(j') for some blocks j != j'. Let P be the
89|runs R_(j+1), ..., R_(j') (cyclically): a nonempty proper set of runs forming one cyclic interval, so z_P changes sign exactly
90|at S_j and S_(j') (m = 1), and T(z_P) = sum over R_i in P of tau_i = y_(j') - y_j = 0. By (SR) with kappa^ = 0 and m = 1,
91|n = 0 is admissible (0 <= 2p-2, 0 <= R), and the empty selection has signed sum 0 = T(z_P). GRX gives w^ an identity
92|2p-subword, contradicting (NF) (w^ avoids). So kappa^ >= 1 (kappa^ >= 0 is accepted). []
93|
94|## 4. Lemma M (pairs), Lemma P, Lemma U
95|
96|Lemma M. A finite multiset S of size N with maximal multiplicity mu contains q pairwise disjoint pairs of elements with
97|different values whenever 2q <= N and q <= N - mu.
98|Proof. Cap every multiplicity at q. The capped total is >= 2q: if mu <= q it is N >= 2q; if mu > q, the top value keeps q and
99|the other values keep a capped total >= min(q, N-mu) = q (it is >= q if one of them has >= q copies, and equals their total
100|N-mu >= q otherwise). Choose a sub-multiset S' of exactly 2q elements within the
101|caps, list S' with equal values consecutive as x_1, ..., x_(2q), and pair x_i with x_(i+q) (1 <= i <= q). If x_i = x_(i+q),
102|that value fills the q+1 consecutive positions i, ..., i+q, against its cap q. []

13|## 1. Lemma CT
14|Let p >= 5 be prime, kappa an integer with 0 <= kappa <= p - 1, Y an odd sequence over C_(2p) with (ZP_kappa), and n an integer with
15|1 <= n <= |Y|, |Y| - n even and 2kappa + 2 <= |Y| - n <= 2p.  Then sum_x min(n, v_x(Y)) <= p + n - 2 (sum over distinct odd x).
16|Proof.  Write odd elements as (1, a) in C_2 x C_p; a -> (1, a) is a bijection, so the C_p-coordinate sequence A has the same
17|multiplicities as Y.  If an n-term subsequence S of Y had sigma_p(S) = sigma_p(Y), then Y - S would have even length |Y| - n and
18|C_p-sum 0, i.e. it would be a zero-sum of length in [2kappa + 2, 2p].  So sigma_p(Y) is not in Sigma_n(A), |Sigma_n(A)| <= p - 1,
19|and RS(a) gives min(p, sum_x min(n, v_x) - n + 1) <= p - 1.  []
